The error is clear:

```
FileNotFoundError: [Errno 2] No such file or directory: '/root/autodl-tmp/models/openvla-7b/proprio_projector--40000_checkpoint.pt'
```

The `load_checkpoint` function looks for the checkpoint in `cfg.vla_path` (which is `/root/autodl-tmp/models/openvla-7b/`), not in the `run_root_dir` checkpoint directory.

The checkpoints are saved in:
`/root/autodl-tmp/checkpoints/detach_ln_v2_s3/openvla-7b+libero_spatial_no_noops+b8+lr-0.0005+lora-r32+dropout-0.0--image_aug--detach_ln_v2_s3--40000_chkpt/`


But the resume code is looking in the wrong place. I need to trace through the finetune.py code to see how `--resume` is supposed to work. The `load_checkpoint` function uses `vla_path` as the base directory, so when resuming, I probably need to point `--vla_path` to the actual checkpoint directory instead of the original model path.

Looking at the `load_checkpoint` function, it constructs the checkpoint path by combining the base path with the module name and resume step, so it's expecting to find files like `proprio_projector--40000_checkpoint.pt` in whatever directory `vla_path` points to. The issue is that the user's instructions say to point `--vla_path` to the original openvla-7b model, but the code is actually looking for checkpoint files there, which don't exist.

If so, set to\\n                                                     #         False and merge final checkpoint offline!\\n\\n    # Logging\\n    wandb_entity: str = \\\"your-wandb-entity\\\"          # Name of WandB entity\\n    wandb_project: str = \\\"your-wandb-project\\\"        # Name of WandB project\\n    run_id_note: Optional[str] = None                # Extra note to add to end of run ID for logging\\n    run_id_override: Optional[str] = None            # Optional string to override the run ID with\\n    wandb_log_freq: int = 10                         # WandB logging frequency in steps\\n\\n    # fmt: on\\n\\n\\ndef remove_ddp_in_checkpoint(state_dict) -> dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Removes the 'module.' prefix from parameter names in a PyTorch model state dictionary that was saved using\\n    DistributedDataParallel (DDP).\\n\\n    When a model is trained using PyTorch's DistributedDataParallel, the saved state dictionary contains parameters\\n    prefixed with 'module.'. This function removes these prefixes to make the state dictionary compatible when\\n    loading into models that are not yet wrapped in DDP.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        state_dict (dict): PyTorch model state dictionary.\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        dict: A new state dictionary with the same contents but with 'module.' prefixes removed from parameter names.\\n              Parameters without the 'module.' prefix remain unchanged.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    new_state_dict = {}\\n    for k, v in state_dict.items():\\n        if k[:7] == \\\"module.\\\":\\n            new_state_dict[k[7:]] = v\\n        else:\\n            new_state_dict[k] = v\\n    return new_state_dict\\n\\n\\ndef get_run_id(cfg) -> str:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Generates or retrieves an identifier string for an experiment run.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        cfg (FinetuneConfig): Training configuration.\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        str: Experiment run ID.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if cfg.run_id_override is not None:\\n        # Override the run ID with the user-provided ID\\n        run_id = cfg.run_id_override\\n    elif cfg.resume:\\n        # Override run ID with the previous resumed run's ID\\n        run_id = cfg.vla_path.split(\\\"/\\\")[-1]\\n        # Remove the \\\"--XXX_chkpt\\\" suffix from the run ID if it exists\\n        if \\\"chkpt\\\" in run_id.split(\\\"--\\\")[-1]:\\n            run_id = \\\"--\\\".join(run_id.split(\\\"--\\\")[:-1])\\n    else:\\n        run_id = (\\n            f\\\"{cfg.vla_path.split('/')[-1]}+{cfg.dataset_name}\\\"\\n            f\\\"+b{cfg.batch_size * cfg.grad_accumulation_steps}\\\"\\n            f\\\"+lr-{cfg.learning_rate}\\\"\\n        )\\n        if cfg.use_lora:\\n            run_id += f\\\"+lora-r{cfg.lora_rank}+dropout-{cfg.lora_dropout}\\\"\\n        if cfg.image_aug:\\n            run_id += \\\"--image_aug\\\"\\n        if cfg.run_id_note is not None:\\n            run_id += f\\\"--{cfg.run_id_note}\\\"\\n    return run_id\\n\\n\\ndef load_checkpoint(module_name: str, path: str, step: int, device: str = \\\"cpu\\\") -> dict:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Loads a checkpoint for a given module.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        module_name (str): Name of model component to load checkpoint for.\\n        path (str): Path to checkpoint directory.\\n        step (int): Gradient step number of saved checkpoint.\\n        device (str): String specifying how to remap storage locations (default = \\\"cpu\\\").\\n\\n    Returns:\\n        dict: PyTorch model state dictionary.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    checkpoint_path = os.path.join(path, f\\\"{module_name}--{step}_checkpoint.pt\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"Loading checkpoint: {checkpoint_path}\\\")\\n    state_dict = torch.load(checkpoint_path, weights_only=True, map_location=device)\\n    return remove_ddp_in_checkpoint(state_dict)\\n\\n\\ndef wrap_ddp(module: nn.Module, device_id: int, find_unused: bool = False) -> DDP:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    Wrap a module with DistributedDataParallel.\\n\\n    Args:\\n        module (nn.Module): PyTorch module.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"}

````text
Now I understand the issue. The `load_checkpoint` function uses `cfg.vla_path` as the directory to look for checkpoint files. When resuming, we need `--vla_path` to point to the checkpoint directory, not the original model.

Looking at the `get_run_id` function when `cfg.resume` is True:
```python
run_id = cfg.vla_path.split("/")[-1]
if "chkpt" in run_id.split("--")[-1]:
    run_id = "--".join(run_id.split("--")[:-1])
```

So when resuming, it extracts the run_id from the vla_path by taking the last directory component and stripping off the checkpoint suffix. The checkpoint directory name like `openvla-7b+libero_spatial_no_noops+b8+lr-0.0005+lora-r32+dropout-0.0--image_aug--detach_ln_v2_s3--40000_chkpt` would have the `--40000_chkpt` suffix removed to recover the original run_id. So I need to set `--vla_path` to point directly to that checkpoint directory.

But wait, I'm realizing this checkpoint directory only contains the LoRA adapter files and head checkpoints, not the full model weights. The model loading code reads from `vla_path`, so I need to trace through the loading logic more carefully to understand what it's actually expecting.

Looking at the checkpoint structure, I see the action head, occupancy head, and proprioceptor projector checkpoints, plus a LoRA adapter subdirectory, but no actual model safetensors files. The `load_checkpoint` function searches for files matching the pattern `{module_name}--{step}_checkpoint.pt` in the checkpoint directory, and when resuming, it tries to load the vision backbone from there. The checkpoint does have config files that would normally let AutoModelForVision2Seq initialize from it, but those safetensors shards are missing—I need to verify whether the user actually has those model weight files in the checkpoint directory. but the actual checkpoint only has LoRA adapter and head checkpoints. This is a LoRA setup, so the checkpoint contains:
1. LoRA adapter weights
2. Head checkpoints (action_head, occ_head, proprio_projector)

The base model (openvla-7b) is loaded separately.

Looking at line 283 more carefully, when `cfg.resume` is true, it calls `load_checkpoint` with `cfg.vla_path`. This is inside `init_module`, so the checkpoint .pt files need to be in `cfg.vla_path`. But I'm realizing the checkpoint structure has a `lora_adapter/` directory plus config and tokenizer files copied from the base model, without any full merged model safetensors. So the loading flow for resume probably needs to load the base model from vla_path first, then layer the LoRA adapter and head checkpoints on top, though the current code seems to be calling `load_checkpoint` with the original model path where the head checkpoints might not actually be located. I'm realizing the checkpoint directory might contain the full merged model weights as safetensors files when `merge_lora_during_training` is enabled, which would explain why the user expected those large model files in the first place.

The issue is likely that either the SIGHUP signal interrupted the save process partway through writing those model shards, or this particular training run was configured to keep LoRA weights separate instead of merging them. Let me look at the 35000 checkpoint to see if it has the complete safetensors files that would indicate a successful save.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I can see:

**35000_chkpt** is complete — it has all 4 safetensors shards plus all head checkpoints.

**40000_chkpt** is incomplete — it has only the head checkpoints and lora_adapter, but the safetensors files were NOT saved (SIGHUP killed the process before the merged model could be saved).

So I have two options:
1. Resume from 35000_chkpt using `--vla_path` pointing to the 35000 checkpoint directory
2. Figure out a way to use the 40000_chkpt without the safetensors
   
For option 2, the issue is that the code loads the model directly from `vla_path` expecting the full safetensors files to be present, so even though 40000_chkpt has the LoRA adapter and head checkpoints, I can't actually load it without the base model weights.

The cleanest approach would be to copy the safetensors from the base openvla-7b model into 40000_chkpt, then point `vla_path` there — but I'd need to verify this works with the LoRA adapter merging logic, since the checkpoint's safetensors represent the merged state at that step, not the base model.

Actually, looking at the time constraint more carefully: the deadline is May 28 at 12:00 UTC and it's already 11:47 UTC, leaving only about 13 minutes. Even resuming from 40000_chkpt would take roughly 2.5 hours for the remaining 10000 steps, which means we're already past the deadline.

Since the user explicitly wants to resume training, I'm considering whether I can reconstruct the missing safetensors files from the checkpoint at step 40000 by loading the base model and extracting the weights from the saved checkpoint files. Resuming from 35000 is the safer choice even though it adds about 1.25 hours—the user mentioned allowing some timeout, and they clearly expected this to run, so I should just proceed with that approach.

For option 2, I'm considering whether I could copy the safetensors and config from 35000 to 40000 and resume from there, but that won't work because the LoRA adapter at 40000 represents the delta from the base model itself, not from the step 35000 state. When `merge_lora_during_training` is enabled, each checkpoint merges the LoRA into the base model before saving, so the safetensors at 35000 wouldn't be compatible with resuming at step 40000.

Rather than dig into the full code logic to understand exactly how model loading works during resume, I'll take the safer route: resume from the 35000 checkpoint using `--vla_path` pointing to it with `--resume_step 35000`. But first, let me check what's actually in the 40000 lora_adapter directory to see if I can construct a working checkpoint from what's there.
```

log_step = gradient_step_idx if not cfg.resume else cfg.resume_step + gradient_step_idx
```

So when resume=True:
- `log_step = resume_step + gradient_step_idx`
- Training stops when `log_step == max_steps`


- Which means it stops when `gradient_step_idx == max_steps - resume_step`

So if I resume from step 35000 with max_steps=50005, I'd train for 15005 more steps (about 3.75 hours at 0.9s per step), but if I resume from 40000, it's only 10005 steps (roughly 2.5 hours). For finishing the training, the cutoff probably doesn't matter since we'd already be past it at either checkpoint. Looking at what's actually in each checkpoint, the 35000 version has several model-related files that aren't in 40000 — the configuration and modeling Python files, generation config, and the actual model weights. But `save_pretrained` should regenerate all those necessary files anyway, so I just need to make sure the config is correct for loading with `AutoModelForVision2Seq`.

Actually, I'm realizing there's a simpler path: I can just copy the safetensors files and config files from 35000_chkpt over to 40000_chkpt. When training resumes from 40000, it'll load the merged model state from 35000 and initialize a fresh LoRA adapter, which is exactly what should happen anyway.

Wait, that won't work—the model and head checkpoints would be out of sync since the heads are at step 40000 but the base model would only be at step 35000. I need to properly merge the LoRA into the base model first, then save that merged state as the new 40000 checkpoint. ```text
The training is running. GPU 3 is at 99% utilization with 63000MiB memory. But I notice something concerning:

1. **GPU 2** also has a process (PID 56474, 62152MiB) — this wasn't there before. This could be from the merge script or another process. Let me check.

Actually, looking again: GPU 2 has PID 56474 and PID 58736. These might be from another training run that was started by someone else, or they could be leftovers. But the user only mentioned cuda:0 having detach_ln_v2_s2. 

I'll note this but leave it alone for now.

2. The logs confirm training has started with the loss values printing out and iterations progressing as expected.

3. There's a potential issue with the progress bar showing `1/50005` instead of `1/10005` — the tqdm total appears to be using `cfg.max_steps` which is 50005, but I need to verify whether the actual stopping condition at line 1309 matches this or if there's a mismatch between what the progress bar displays and when training actually terminates.

Looking more closely at the math: if `log_step = cfg.resume_step + gradient_step_idx` where `cfg.resume_step = 40000`, then the stop condition triggers when `log_step = 50005`, meaning `gradient_step_idx = 10005`. So the training will run for about 10005 steps total, but the progress bar will only show it reaching 20% completion visually, even though the actual duration (~2.5 hours) is correct.

The action loss value of 0.1299 in the initial output seems reasonable for the start of training.

GPU 3 is running the resumed training (PID 56822), and I'm noticing some GPU 2 processes that appear to be from a different experiment. The training has successfully resumed from step 40000.
```
